"(b) 코코아두를 갈은 페이스트(paste)(제1803호)\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 18.01]\n\n18.01 Cocoa beans, whole or broken, raw or roasted.\n\nCocoa beans are the seeds, contained in large numbers (25 to 80), in the fruit (cocoa pod) of the cacao tree (Theobroma cacao). They are of flat ovoid form, generally violet or reddish in colour. They consist of a tough, brittle shell, and a very thin whitish inner husk or skin covering the kernel and dividing it into several sections.\n\nIn order to reduce their slightly bitter taste, to develop the aroma and to facilitate shelling, the beans are fermented; they may alternatively be steam treated and dried. They are roasted to facilitate removal of the shells, to render the kernels more friable, to concentrate the product and improve the flavour and aroma. They are then passed through corrugated rollers which break up the beans and detach the germs; subsequent processes separate the shells, husks and germs from the broken pieces of kernels (cocoa nibs).\n\nThe heading covers raw or roasted beans, whole (whether or not separated from their shells, husks, skins or germs) or broken.\n\nThe heading does not include :\n\n(a) Shells, husks, skins and other cocoa waste (heading 18.02).\n\n(b) Cocoa beans ground to paste (heading 18.03)."
"(b) Cocoa beans ground to paste (heading 18.03).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n18.02 - 코코아의 껍데기와 껍질, 그 밖의 코코아 웨이스트(waste)\n\n이 호에는 코코아 분말이나 코코아 버터를 제조할 때 생긴 웨이스트(waste)를 분류한다. 이 잔유물의 일부는 코코아 버터를 더 추출하는데 사용하고 이들 모두는 테오브로민(theobromine)의 추출에 사용될 수 있다. 또한 동물사료에 소량이 첨가될 수 있다. 잘게 부순 것은 향미(香味)는 다르나 냄새는 코코아와 유사하며 코코아 가루 대용물로 사용하는 경우도 있다.\n\n이 호에는 다음의 물품을 포함한다.\n\n(1) 껍데기와 껍질(shell, husk and skin) : 두(bean)를 볶고 부수는 공정에서 분리한다. 이들은 소량의 핵의 파편(껍데기와 껍질에 붙어 있어 용이하게 분리되지 않는 것)을 함유하고 있으며, 이것으로부터 코코아 버터를 어느 정도 추출할 수 있다.\n\n(2) 코코아의 씨눈(germ) : 이것은 코코아두를 소위 씨눈제거기(de-germing machine)에 통과시켜서 얻는다. 이들은 거의 지방을 함유하지 않는다.\n\n(3) 선별기에서 껍데기(shell)를 세척하는 과정에서 생겨나는 코코아 더스트(dust) : 보통 이들의 지방 함유량은 추출하기에 경제적으로 합당할 정도로 충분하다.\n\n(4) 코코아 케이크[핵의 조각을 함유하는 껍데기와 껍질의 웨이스트(waste)나 원형의 두(whole bean)에서 코코아 버터를 추출한 후 남은 잔유물] : 이 케이크는 껍데기와 껍질의 미립자를 함유하고 있으므로 코코아 가루(powder)나 초콜릿의 제조에 부적당하다.\n\n이 호에서는 코코아 페이스트(paste)에서 코코아 버터를 추출한 결과인, 껍데기와 껍질이 없는 코코아케이크를 제외한다(제1803호).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 18.02]\n\n18.02 Cocoa s
<truncated 1548 bytes>
"The heading excludes cocoa cake free from shells, husks and skins, resulting from the extraction of cocoa butter from cocoa paste (heading 18.03).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n18.03 - 코코아 페이스트(paste)[탈지(脫脂)한 것인지에 상관없다]\n\n1803.10 - 탈지(脫脂)하지 않은 것\n\n1803.20 - 전부나 일부를 탈지(脫脂)한 것\n\n코코아 페이스트(paste)는 볶은 코코아두[껍데기와 껍질(shell, husk and skin)과 씨눈(germs)를 제거한 것]를 가열한 그라인드스톤(grindstone)이나 원판 파쇄기(disc crusher)로 잘게 부수어 얻는다. 이 물품은 태블릿(tablet) 모양ㆍ덩어리 모양ㆍ블록 모양으로 응고된다. 이 페이스트(paste)는 이 상태로 과자제조자가 사용할 수 있으나, 보통 코코아 버터ㆍ코코아 가루와 초콜릿 제조에 사용한다.\n\n이러한 페이스트(paste)는 일부나 전부 탈지(脫脂)한 페이스트(paste)를 분류하며(코코아 케이크), 이는 코코아 가루나 초콜릿 제조에 사용하거나, 어떤 경우에는 테오브로민(theobromine) 제조에 사용한다.\n\n이 호에서는 설탕이나 그 밖의 감미료를 함유하고 있는 코코아 페이스트(cocoa paste)를 제외한다(제1806호).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 18.03]\n\n18.03 Cocoa paste, whether or not defatted.\n\n1803.10 Not defatted\n\n1803.20 Wholly or partly defatted\n\nCocoa paste is obtained by grinding roasted cocoa beans (cleaned of their shells, husks, skins and germs) between heated grindstones or disc crushers; the resulting product is solidified in tablets, lumps or blocks. The paste can be used in this state by confectioners but it is generally used for the manufacture of cocoa butter, cocoa powder and chocolate.\n\nThe heading also covers paste which has been wholly or partly defatted (cocoa cake); this is used for the manufacture of cocoa powder or chocolate, or in some cases for the manufacture of theo

"The heading excludes cocoa paste containing added sugar or other sweetening matter (heading 18.06).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n18.04 - 코코아 버터, 지방이나 기름\n\n코코아 버터(cocoa butter)는 코코아두에 함유된 지방 물질로서, 보통 코코아 페이스트(paste)나 원형의 두(whole bean)를 열간압착하여 얻는다. 또한 하급품은 흔히 코코아 지방(脂)이라고 불리는데, 상한 코코아두나 여러 가지의 코코아 웨이스트(waste)[껍데기ㆍ껍질ㆍ더스트(dust) 등]를 압착하거나 적당한 용제(solvent)로 추출하여 얻는다.\n\n코코아 버터는 보통 실온(室溫)에서 고체이며 약간 기름 상태로서 황백색이다. 이는 코코아와 유사한 냄새가 나며 유쾌한 향미(香味 : flavour)가 있다. 보통 슬래브(slab) 모양으로 제시하는데 초콜릿 제조[코코아 페이스트(paste)를 강화]ㆍ과자의 제조[특정 단과자(sweets)를 조제하기 위하여]ㆍ향료[냉침법(冷浸法)에 의한 향료 추출]ㆍ화장품류와 의료용품[연고(ointment)ㆍ좌약(suppository) 등]의 제조에 사용한다.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 18.04]\n\n18.04 Cocoa butter, fat and oil.\n\nCocoa butter, the fatty matter contained in cocoa beans, is generally obtained by hot pressing either cocoa paste or the whole bean. An inferior quality, often referred to as cocoa fat, can also be obtained from spoiled cocoa beans or from various kinds of cocoa waste (shells, husks, dust, etc.) either by pressure or by extraction with suitable solvents.\n\nCocoa butter is generally solid at room temperature, slightly oily and yellowish white in colour; it has an odour similar to that of cocoa and an agreeable flavour. It is generally presented in slabs, and is used in chocolate making (to enrich cocoa pastes), in confectionery (for the preparation of certain sweets), in perfumery (for extracting perfumes by the enfleurage process), i
<truncated 104 bytes>
"Cocoa butter is generally solid at room temperature, slightly oily and yellowish white in colour; it has an odour similar to that of cocoa and an agreeable flavour. It is generally presented in slabs, and is used in chocolate making (to enrich cocoa pastes), in confectionery (for the preparation of certain sweets), in perfumery (for extracting perfumes by the enfleurage process), in the manufacture of cosmetics and in pharmacy (for the preparation of ointments, suppositories, etc.).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n18.05 - 코코아 가루(설탕이나 그 밖의 감미료를 첨가한 것은 제외한다)\n\n코코아 가루(cocoa powder)는 제1803호에서 설명된 코코아 페이스트(paste)를 부분적으로 탈지(脫脂 : defatted)하고 가루화하여 얻는다.\n\n이 호에는 설탕이나 그 밖의 감미료를 첨가하지 않은 코코아 가루만을 분류한다. 이 호에는 특히 니브(nib)ㆍ페이스트(paste)ㆍ가루를 그것의 용해도(solubility)를 증가시키기 위하여 알칼리 물질(탄산나트륨ㆍ탄산칼륨 등)로 처리하여 얻은 코코아 가루(가용성 코코아)를 포함한다.\n\n설탕이나 그 밖의 감미료를 첨가한 코코아 가루와 분유나 펩톤을 첨가한 코코아 가루는 제1806호에 해당한다. 그러나 의약품 안에 들어 있는 코코아 가루가 단지 의약품의 지지물(support)이나 매체(vehicle)로 인정되는 것은 제3003호나 제3004호에 해당한다.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 18.05]\n\n18.05 Cocoa powder, not containing added sugar or other sweetening matter.\n\nCocoa powder is obtained by pulverising the partly defatted cocoa paste referred to in heading 18.03.\n\nThis heading covers only cocoa powder not containing added sugar or other sweetening matter. The drops form small pellets or flakes which are sometimes crushed or granulated. In another method, the starch paste is agglomerated in a steam heated vessel.\n\nThe products are marketed in the form of flakes, grains, pearls, siftings, seeds or similar forms. They are used for the preparation of soups, puddings or dietetic foods.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n19.04 - 곡물이나 곡물 가공품을 팽창시키거나 볶아서 얻은 조제 식료품[예: 콘 플레이크(corn flake)]과 낱알 모양이나 플레이크(flake) 모양이나 그 밖의 가공한 곡물(옥수수는 제외하며 고운 가루ㆍ부순 알곡ㆍ거친 가루는 제외하고 사전조리나 그 밖의 방법으로 조제한 것으로서 따로 분류되지 않은 것으로 한정한다)\n\n1904.10 - 곡물이나 곡물가공품을 팽창시키거나 볶아서 얻은 조제 식료품\n\n1904.20 - 볶지 않은 곡물 플레이크(flake)의 조제 식료품ㆍ볶지 않은 곡물 플레이크(flake)와 볶은 곡물 플레이크(flake)나 팽창된 곡물의 혼합물로 만든 조제 식료품\n\n1904.30 - 불거 밀(bulgur wheat)\n\n1904.90 - 기타

They are mainly used, with or without milk, as breakfast foods. Salt, sugar, molasses, malt extract, fruit or cocoa (see Note 3 and the General Explanatory Note to this Chapter), etc., may have been added during or after their manufacture.\n\nThe group also includes similar foodstuffs obtained, by swelling or roasting, from flour or bran.\n\nCorn flakes are made from grains of maize by removing the pericarp and the germ, adding sugar, salt and malt extract, softening with steam and then rolling into flakes and roasting in a rotary oven. The same process may be applied to wheat or other cereal grains.\n\n“Puffed” rice and wheat also fall in this group. These products are prepared by subjecting the grains to pressure in a moist, heated chamber. Sudden removal of the pressure and ejection into a cold atmosphere causes the grain to expand to several times its original volume.\n\nThis group further includes crisp savoury food products, obtained by submitting moistened cereal grains (whole

"건조과실(예: 대추야자와 프룬)은 소량의 당을 첨가한 것이나 과실의 겉에 건조로 인하여 자연적으로 생긴 당이 덮여 있는 것은 이 호의 설탕에 절인 과실과 외관이 약간 유사하더라도 제8류에 분류한다.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 20.06]\n\n20.06 Vegetables, fruit, nuts, fruit-peel and other parts of plants, preserved by sugar (drained, glacé or crystallised).\n\nThe products of this heading are prepared first by treating the vegetables, fruit, nuts, fruit peel or other parts of plants with boiling water (which softens the material and facilitates penetration of the sugar), and then by repeated heating to boiling point and storage in syrups of progressively increasing sugar concentration until they are sufficiently impregnated with sugar to ensure their preservation.\n\nThe principal products preserved by sugar are whole fruit or nuts (cherries, apricots, pears, plums, chestnuts (marrons glacés), walnuts, etc.), sections or pieces of fruit (oranges, lemons, pineapples, etc.), fruit peel (citron, lemon, orange, melon, etc.), other parts of plants (angelica, ginger, yams, sweet potatoes, etc.) and flowers (violets, mimosa, etc.).\n\nDrained products are prepared by using a syrup (e.g., a mixture of invert sugar or glucose with a proportion of sucrose) which does not crystallise on exposure to the air. After impregnation the excess syrup is drained off leaving the product sticky to the touch.\n\nGlacé products are obtained by dipping the drained product in a sucrose syrup which dries as a thin, shiny coating.\n\nCrystallised products are prepared by allowing the sucrose syrup to penetrate into the product so that, on drying, it forms crystals on the surface or throughout the product.\n\nThose goods preserved by sugar and put up in syrup, whatever the packing, are excluded from this heading (heading 20.02, 20.03 or 20.05, in the case of vegetables, or heading 20.08, in the case of fruit, nuts, fruit-p
<truncated 366 bytes>

When cool they are of moderately firm consistency and contain pieces of the fruit.\n\nMarmalades are a variety of jam generally prepared from citrus fruit.\n\nFruit jellies are prepared by boiling fruit juices (expressed from raw or cooked fruit) with sugar until the product sets on cooling. They are of firm consistency, clear and free from pieces of fruit.\n\nFruit or nut purées are prepared by boiling sieved fruit pulp or powdered nuts with or without the addition of sugar, to a thickish consistency. Fruit purées differ from jams in having a higher proportion of fruit and a smoother consistency.\n\nFruit or nut pastes (apple, quince, pear, apricot, almond, etc.) are evaporated purées of a solid or almost solid consistency.\n\nProducts of this heading which are normally prepared with sugar may be sweetened with synthetic agents (e.g., sorbitol) instead of sugar.\n\nThis heading also includes homogenised preparations.\n\nThe heading excludes :\n\n(a) Jellies and pastes in the form of sugar confectionery or chocolate confectionery (heading 17.04 or 18.06 respectively).\n\n(b) Table jellies prepared from gelatin, sugar and fruit juice or artificial fruit essences (heading 21.06).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n20.08 - 그 밖의 방법으로 조제하거나 보존처리한 과실ㆍ견과류와 그 밖의 식

<truncated 180 bytes>
"3.- For the purposes of heading 21.04, the expression “homogenised composite food preparations” means preparations consisting of a finely homogenised mixture of two or more basic ingredients such as meat, fish, vegetables, fruit or nuts, put up for retail sale as food suitable for infants or young children or for dietetic purposes, in containers of a net weight content not exceeding 250 g. For the application of this definition, no account is to be taken of small quantities of any ingredients which may be added to the mixture for seasoning, preservation or other purposes. Sauces are generally in liquid form and preparations for sauces are usually in the form of powders to which only milk, water, etc. need to be added to obtain a sauce.\n\nSauces are normally added to a food as it cooks or as it is served. Sauces provide flavour, moisture, and a contrast in texture and colour. They may also serve as a medium in which food is contained, for example, the velouté sauce of creamed chicken. Seasoning liquids (soy sauce, hot pepper sauce, fish sauce) are used both as ingredients in cooking and at table as condiments.\n\nThe heading also includes certain preparations, based on vegetables or fruit, which are mainly liquids, emulsions or suspensions, and sometimes contain visible pieces of vegetables or fruit. Alcoholic beverages are classified in headings 22.03 to 22.06 or heading 22.08 as appropriate.\n\n°\n\n° °\n\nSubheading Note.\n\n1.- For the purposes of subheading 2204.10, the expression “sparkling wine” means wine which, when kept at a temperature of 20 °C in closed containers, has an excess pressure of not less than 3 bars.\n\nGENERAL\n\nThe products of this Chapter constitute a group quite distinct from the foodstuffs covered by the preceding Chapters of the Nomenclature.\n\nThey fall into four main groups :\n\n(A) Water and other non-alcoholic beverages and ice.\n\n(B) Fermented alcoholic beverages (beer, wine, cider, etc.).\n\n(C) Distilled alcoholic liquids and beverages (liqueurs, spirits, etc.) and ethyl alcohol.\n\n(D) Vinegar and substitutes for vinegar.\n\nThis Chapter does not cover :\n\n(a) Liquid dairy products of Chapter 4.\n\n(b) Products of this Chapter (other than those of heading 22.09) prepared for culinary purposes and thereby rendered unsuitable for consumption

"(d) Medicaments of heading 30.03 or 30.04.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n22.03 - 맥주\n\n맥주는 발아한 곡물(가장 일반적으로 보리와 밀)ㆍ물과 홉(hop)으로 조제한 리큐어(liquor)(맥아즙)를 발효하여 얻는 알코올 음료이다. 발아하지 않은 곡물(예: 옥수수나 쌀)의 일정량이 리큐어(liquor)(맥아즙)의 조제를 위해서 사용하기도 한다. 홉의 첨가는 쓴 맛과 향미(香味)를 주고 품질 보존성을 증대시킨다. 때로는 체리나 그 밖의 향미료(香味料)를 발효 중에 첨가한다.\n\n당(당)(특히 포도당)ㆍ착색제ㆍ이산화탄소나 그 밖의 물질이 첨가된 경우도 있다.\n\n발효방법에 따라 하면효모(bottom yeast)를 가지고 저온에서 얻은 하면발효(bottom fermentation) 맥주와 상면효모(top yeast)를 가지고 고온에서 얻은 상면발효(top fermentation) 맥주가 있다.\n\n맥주는 담색의 것이나 흑색의 것ㆍ단 맛의 것이나 쓴 맛의 것ㆍ독하지 않은 것이나 독한 것도 있다. 이것은 술통ㆍ병ㆍ밀폐 금속통에 포장된 것도 있으며, 에일(ale)ㆍ스타우트(stout) 등으로 시판한다.\n\n또한 이 호에는 농축한 맥주(concentrated beer)도 분류하는데, 이는 낮은 알코올 도수를 가진(그러나 맥아 추출물의 함량은 높은) 맥주를 원래 용량의 5분의 1에서 6분의 1 사이가 되도록 진공-농축해서 조제한다.\n\n이 호에는 다음의 것은 제외한다.\n\n(a) 때때로 맥주로 호칭되지만 알코올을 함유하지 않은 어떤 음료(예: 물과 캐러멜화한 설탕으로 제조된 음료)(제2202호)\n\n(b) 맥아로 제조한 맥주로 구성된 것으로서 알코올을 함유하지 않은 맥주로 불리는 음료. 이것의 알코올 용량은 전 용량의 0.5% 이하로 감소된 것이다(제2202호).\n\n(c) 제3003호나 제3004호의 의약품\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 22.03

"(c) Medicaments of heading 30.03 or 30.04.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n22.04 - 포도주(생포도로 제조한 것으로 한정하며, 알코올로 강화한 포도주를 포함한다)와 포도즙(제2009호의 것은 제외한다)\n\n2204.10 - 발포성(發泡性) 포도주\n\n- 그 밖의 포도주와 알코올 첨가에 따라 발효를 중지한 포도즙\n\n2204.21 - 2리터 이하의 용기에 넣은 것\n\n2204.22 - 2리터 초과 10리터 이하의 용기에 넣은 것\n\n2204.29 - 기타\n\n2204.30 - 그 밖의 포도즙\n\n(Ⅰ) 포도주(생포도로 제조한 것으로 한정한다)\n\n이 호에 분류하는 포도주는 신선한 포도의 착즙을 발효시켜 얻은 알코올성의 최종 물품이다.\n\n이 호에는 다음의 것을 포함한다.\n\n(1) 보통 포도주(적색ㆍ백색ㆍ장미색)\n\n(2) 알코올로 강화한 포도주\n\n(3) 발포성(發泡性) 포도주(sparkling wine) : 이들 포도주는 밀폐한 용기 내에서 최종 발효를 유도하거나(발포성 포도주에 적당한), 병에 주입 후 인공적으로 가스를 첨가하는 방법(탄산가스함유 포도주)에 의해, 이산화탄소가 충전되어 있는 것이다.\n\n(4) 디저트 포도주(dessert wine)[때로는 리큐르 포도주(liqueur wine)라고 부른다] : 이 포도주는 알코올분이 많으며 일반적으로 당분의 함량이 많은 포도즙(이것의 일부만이 발효에 의해 알코올로 변환된다)에서 얻어진다. 어떤 경우에서는 알코올을 첨가하거나, 알코올을 첨가하여 농축한 포도즙을 첨가하여 강화시킨다. 디저트(또는 리큐르) 포도주에는 특히 다음의 것을 포함한다. 즉, 카나리아(Canary)ㆍ사이프러스(Cyprus)ㆍ라크리마 크리스티(Lacryma Christi)ㆍ머디러(Madeira)ㆍ말라가(Malaga)ㆍ말름시(Malmsey)ㆍ마르살라(Marsala)ㆍ포트(Port)ㆍ세이모스(Samos)와 셰리(Sherry)이다.\n\n이 호에는 다음의 것은 제외한다.\n\n(a) 제2205호에 해

The main use of most of these products is as animal feeding stuffs, either alone or mixed with other materials, although some of them are fit for human consumption. Certain products (e.g., wine lees, argol, oil-cake) also have industrial uses.\n\nReferences in this Chapter to “pellets” mean products which have been agglomerated either directly by compression or by the addition of a binder (molasses, starchy substances, etc.) in a proportion not exceeding 3 % by weight.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n23.01 - 육ㆍ설육(屑肉)ㆍ어류ㆍ갑각류ㆍ연체동물이나 그 밖의 수생(水生) 무척추동물의 고운 가루ㆍ거친 가루ㆍ펠릿(pellet)(식용에 적합하지 않은 것으로 한정한다)과 수지박\n\n2301.10 - 육이나 설육(屑肉)의 고운 가루ㆍ거친 가루ㆍ펠릿(pellet)과 수지박\n\n2301.20 - 어류ㆍ갑각류ㆍ연체동물이나 그 밖의 수

The resultant product is then dried and sterilised by prolonged heating, and finally ground.\n\nThe heading also covers the above products in the form of pellets (see the General Explanatory Note to this Chapter).\n\nThe flours, meals and pellets of this heading are used mainly in animal feeding, but may also be used for other purposes (e.g., as fertilisers).\n\nThis heading excludes flours and meals of insects, unfit for human consumption (heading 05.11).\n\n(2) Greaves, the membraneous tissues remaining after pig or other animal fats have been rendered. They are used for animal fodder or as fertilisers; some of these residues (e.g., maize steeping liquors) are used in the production of cultures for the manufacture of antibiotics.\n\n(B) Beet pulp is the residue which remains after the sugar has been extracted from the root of the sugar beet. This pulp is classified in this heading whether wet or dried but, if with added molasses or otherwise prepared as animal food, it falls in heading 23.09.\n\n(C) Bagasse is a residue consisting of the fibrous portion of the sugar cane after the juice has been extracted. It is used in the paper making industry and in the preparation of animal food.\n\n(D) Other waste products of sugar manufacture covered by this heading include defecation scum, filter press residues, etc.\n\n(E) Brewing or distilling dregs and waste comprise in particular :\n\n(1) Dregs of cereals (barley, rye, etc.), obtained in the manufacture of beer and consisting of the exhausted grains remaining after the wort has been drawn off.\n\n(2) Malt sprouts separated from the malted grain during the kilning process.\n\n(3) Spent hops.\n\n(4) Dregs resulting from the distillation of spirits from grain, seeds, potatoes, etc.\n\n(5) Beet pulp wash (residues from the distillation of beet molasses).\n\n(Al

However, tobacco-free products intended for smoking in a water pipe are excluded from this subheading.\n\nGENERAL\n\nTobacco is obtained from various cultivated varieties of the genus Nicotiana of the Solanaceae family. The size and shape of the leaves differ from one variety to another.\n\nThe harvesting method and curing process depend on the variety (type) of tobacco. The plant may be cut whole, at average maturity (stalk cutting), or the leaves may be picked separately, according to their state of maturity (priming). Thus, tobacco may be cured either as whole plants (on the stalk) or as separate leaves.\n\nThe various methods of curing are sun curing (in the open air), air curing (in closed sheds with free circulation of air), flue curing (in hot air flues), or fire curing (with open fires).\n\nBefore packing for shipment, the dried leaves

However, products such as cannabis are excluded (heading 12.11).\n\n(6) “Homogenised” or “reconstituted” tobacco made by agglomerating finely divided tobacco from tobacco leaves, tobacco refuse or dust, whether or not on a backing (e.g., sheet of cellulose from tobacco stems), generally put up in the form of rectangular sheets or strip. It can be either used in the sheet form (as a wrapper) or shredded/chopped (as a filler).\n\n(7) Tobacco extracts and essences. These are liquids extracted from moist leaves by pressure, or prepared by boiling waste tobacco in water. Salt is used for culinary purposes (cooking salt, table salt), but it also has many other uses and, if necessary, may be denatured to render it unfit for human consumption.\n\nThe heading includes :\n\n(A) Salt which is extracted from underground :\n\neither by conventional mining (rock salt),\n\nor by solution mining (water is injected under pressure into a layer of salt and returns to the surface as saturated brine).\n\n(B) Evaporated salt :\n\nsolar salt (sea salt) is obtained by evaporation of sea water by the sun;\n\nrefined salt is obtained by evaporation of saturated brine.\n\n(C) Sea water, brine and other saline solutions.\n\nThe heading also covers :\n\n(1) Salt (e.g., table salt) which has been slightly iodised, phosphated, etc., or treated so that it will remain dry.\n\n(2) Salt to which anti-caking agents or free-flowing agents have been added.\n\n(3) Salt which has been denatured by any process.\n\n(4) Residuary sodium chloride, in particular that left after chemical processing (e.g., electrolysis) or obtained as a by-product of the treatment of certain ores.\n\nThis heading does not include :\n\n(a) Salted condiments such as celery salt (heading 21.03).\n\n(b) Sodium chloride solutions, including sea water, put up in ampoules, and sodium chloride otherwise put up as medicaments (Chapter 30), and sodium chloride solutions put up for hygiene use in packings for retail sale, other than medical or pharmaceutical, whether or not sterile (heading 33.07).\n\n(c) Cultured sodium chloride crystals weighing not less than 2.5 g each (other than optical elements) (heading 38.24).\n\n(d) Optical elements of sodium chloride (heading 90

Their “apparent specific gravity”, which must not exceed 1, is to be taken as their effective weight in kg/1,000 cm3, uncompressed, in the form in which they are presented.\n\nThe principal siliceous earths are kieselguhr, tripolite, diatomite and moler earth. Although certain earths classified here are sometimes referred to as “tripoli”, they must not be confused with the true tripoli known as “rotten stone”, which, since it results from the natural decomposition of certain rocks, is not diatomaceous. This latter product, which is used as a mild abrasive for polishing, falls in heading 25.13.\n\nThe various earths of this heading are sometimes incorrectly called “infusorial earths”.\n\nMost of these earths are used for the manufacture of heat resisting or heat or sound insulating articles of heading 68.06 or 69.01. Marble is usually variously tinted by the presence of mineral oxides (coloured veined marble, onyx marble, etc.), but there are pure white varieties.\n\nTravertines are varieties of calcareous stone containing layers of open cells.\n\nEcaussine is extracted from various quarries in Belgium and particularly at Ecaussines. It is a bluish grey stone with an irregular crystalline structure and contains many fossilised shells. On fracture Ecaussine shows a granular surface similar to granite and is therefore sometimes known as “Belgian granite”, “Flanders granite” or “petit granit”.\n\nThe heading covers other similar hard calcareous monumental or building stones, provided their apparent specific gravity is 2.5 or more (i.e., effective weight in kg/1,000 cm3). Calcareous monumental or building stones of an apparent specific gravity of less than 2.5 are classified in heading 25.16.\n\nThe heading also includes both gypseous alabaster, which is usually white and uniformly translucent, and calcareous alabaster, normally yellowish and veined.\n\nThe heading is restricted

"제2515.12호의 해설을 참조한다.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 25.16]\n\n25.16 Granite, porphyry, basalt, sandstone and other monumental or building stone, whether or not roughly trimmed or merely cut, by sawing or otherwise, into blocks or slabs of a rectangular (including square) shape (+).\n\n- Granite :\n\n2516.11 - Crude or roughly trimmed\n\n2516.12 - Merely cut, by sawing or otherwise, into blocks or slabs of a rectangular (including square) shape\n\n2516.20 - Sandstone\n\n2516.90 - Other monumental or building stone\n\nGranite is a very hard, granular igneous rock formed by the agglomeration of quartz crystals with feldspar and mica. It varies in colour (grey, green, pink, red, etc.) according to the relative proportions of these three substances and the presence of iron oxide or manganese oxide.\n\nPorphyry is a finely grained, slightly translucent variety of granite.\n\nSandstone is a rock of sedimentary origin composed of small quartzose or siliceous particles naturally agglomerated by calcareous or siliceous materials.\n\nBasalt is also an igneous rock, blackish, very compact and extremely hard.\n\nThe heading also includes other hard igneous rocks (e.g., syenite, gneiss, trachyte, lava, diabase, diorite, phonolite), as well as calcareous monumental or building stone not falling in heading 25.15 (including building limestone or Portland stone) and serpentine marble (or ophite) which, being a natural form of magnesium silicate, cannot be classified in heading 25.15.\n\nThe stones of this heading may be shaped or processed in the same ways as the stones of heading 25.15 (see the Explanatory Note to that heading). Dolomite is calcined at a temperature range of 700 °C - 1000 C to convert it into magnesium and calcium oxides by releasing carbon dioxide. On the other hand, sintered dolomite is obtained by heating dolomite to a temperature range of 1700 °C - 1900 C when it becomes a refractory material. The heading also includes dolomite which has been roughly trimmed or merely cut, by sawing or otherwise, into blocks or slabs of a rectangular (including square) shape.\n\nHowever, the heading does not cover crushed dolomite for concrete aggregates, road metalling or railway ballast (heading 25.17) or dolomite ramming mix (heading 38.16).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n25.19 - 천연 탄산마그네슘(마그네사이트), 용융(溶融) 마그네시아, 소결(燒結)한 마그네시아[소결(燒結)하기 전에 첨가된 기타 산화물을 소량 함유한 것인지에 상관없다], 그 밖의 산화마그네슘(순수한 것인지에 상관없다)\n\n2519.10 - 천연 탄산마그네슘(마그네사이트)\n\n2519.90 - 기타\n\n이 호에 분류하는 마그네사이트(magnesite)[또는 지오버타이트(giobertite)]는 천연적으로 생긴 탄산마그네슘으로 불순물의 함유 비율이 여러 가지가 있다.\n\n이 호에는 또한 천

"The heading excludes purified calcium oxide and calcium hydroxide (heading 28.25).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n25.23 - 포틀랜드(Portland) 시멘트ㆍ알루미나(aluminous) 시멘트ㆍ슬래그(slag) 시멘트ㆍ슈퍼설페이트(super sulphate) 시멘트와 이와 유사한 수경성(水硬性) 시멘트[착색한 것인지 또는 클링커(clinker) 형태로 된 것인지에 상관없다](+)\n\n2523.10 - 시멘트 클링커(clinker)\n\n- 포틀랜드(Portland) 시멘트\n\n2523.21 - 백색 시멘트(인위적으로 착색한 것인지에 상관없다)\n\n2523.29 - 기타\n\n2523.30 - 알루미나(aluminous) 시멘트\n\n2523.90 - 그 밖의 수경성(水硬性) 시멘트\n\n포틀랜드 시멘트(Portland cement)는 천연의 상태나 인공적으로 혼합한 상태에서 적당한 비율의 점토(clay)를 함유하고 있는 석회석을 소성(firing)하여 만든다. 실리카(silica)ㆍ알루미나(alumina)ㆍ철과 같은 물질을 함유하고 있는 그 밖의 재료가 첨가될 수도 있다. 소성 공정의 결과로 클링커(clinker)라고 하는 반제품(semi-finished product)이 만들어진다. 이러한 클링커(clinker)는 다시 포틀랜드 시멘트를 생산하기 위하여 잘게 부숴 지는데, 이들은 시멘트의 물 속에서 경화되는 특성을 조정하는 첨가제와 촉진제(accelerator)를 섞는다. 포틀랜드 시멘트의 주요한 종류로는 보통 포틀랜드 시멘트ㆍ중용(mederate) 포틀랜드 시멘트ㆍ백색 포틀랜드 시멘트와 같은 것이 있다.\n\n이 호에는 알루미나(aluminous) 시멘트ㆍ슬래그(slag) 시멘트ㆍ슈퍼설페이트(supersulphate) 시멘트[용광로 슬래그(slag)에 소석고와 경화촉진제를 혼합하여 잘게 부순 것]ㆍ포조라나 시멘트(pozzolana cement)ㆍ로만 시멘트(Roman cement) 등과 앞에서 열거한 시멘트의 혼합물을 분류한다.\n\n이 호의 시멘트는 착색될 수도 있다.\n\n이 호에서는 시멘트라는 이름으로

Other materials such as silica, alumina or iron bearing substances may also be added. As a result of the firing process, semi-finished products known as clinkers are obtained. These clinkers are subsequently ground to produce Portland cement, which may incorporate additives and accelerators to modify its hydraulic properties. The principal types of Portland cement are normal Portland cement, moderate Portland cement and white Portland cement.\n\nThe heading also covers aluminous cement, slag cement, supersulphate cement (ground blast furnace slag mixed with an accelerator and calcined gypsum), pozzolana cement, Roman cement, etc., and mixtures of the above mentioned cements.\n\nThe cements of this heading may be coloured.\n\nThis heading does not include certain products sometimes known under the name of cement, such as, Keene’s cement or English cement (alumed plaster gypsum) (heading 25.20), and pozzolana earth, santorin earth and similar substances, sometimes called natural cements (heading 25.30).\n\nThe head

"질석(蛭石 : vermiculte)(운모와 유사한 광물)과 진주석(perlite)ㆍ녹니석(綠泥石 : chlorite)[질석(蛭石)과 화학적으로 관련된 광물]은 제2530호에 해당한다.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 25.25]\n\n25.25 Mica, including splittings; mica waste.\n\n2525.10 Crude mica and mica rifted into sheets or splittings\n\n2525.20 Mica powder\n\n2525.30 Mica waste\n\nMica (muscovite, phlogopite, biotite, etc.) constitutes a group of natural complex aluminium silicates characterised by the fact that they are readily split into glistening, transparent, flexible sheets of varied colour.\n\nThe heading includes :\n\n(A) Crude mica, which consists of mica crystals, of irregular shape, size and thickness, covered with earth (“books”).\n\n(B) Mica sheets, obtained by rifting cobbed and trimmed books. The sheets take the shape of irregular polygons, like the crystals from which they were obtained, and their edges are roughly trimmed and bevelled. Their thickness usually varies from 200 to 750 micrometres (microns).\n\n(C) Mica splittings, obtained by rifting sheet mica. Like the sheets from which they have been rifted, they have the shape of irregular polygons. Their edges are roughly trimmed.\n\nThey are marketed as :\n\n(1) Condenser film, usually of a thickness between 25 and 200 micrometres (microns), or\n\n(2) Splittings, usually of a thickness between 12 and 30 micrometres (microns), used solely for the manufacture of built up mica (e.g., micanite).\n\nThe heading also includes mica waste and powder.\n\nThe heading excludes products obtained by cutting out or die stamping from mica sheets or splittings (heading 68.14 or Chapter 85), and products made from bonded (built up) splittings (e.g., micanite, micafolium) or from pulped (reconstituted) mica (heading 68.14).\n\nVermiculite, a mineral allied to mica, falls in heading 25.30, as do perlite and the chlorites (minerals chemically related to vermiculite)."
"Vermiculite, a mineral allied to mica, falls in heading 25.30, as do perlite and the chlorites (minerals chemically related to vermiculite).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n25.26 - 천연 동석(凍石)[톱질이나 그 밖의 방법으로 거칠게 다듬거나 단순히 절단하여 직사각형(정사각형을 포함한다)의 블록 모양이나 슬래브 모양으로 한 것인지에 상관없다]과 활석\n\n2526.10 - 부수지 않은 것으로 가루도 아닌 것\n\n2526.20 - 부수거나 가루인 것\n\n천연 동석(凍石 : steatite)과 활석(talc)은 함수 규산마그네슘이 풍부하게 함유하고 있는 광물성 물질이다. 천연 동석(凍石)은 활석에 비하여 조밀하고 덩어리 모양이며 활석은 만지면 엷은 조각으로 떨어지며 보다 더 부드럽고 보다 더 미끈거리는 특성을 가지고 있다.\n\n이 호에 해당하는 천연 동석(凍石) : 제2515호의 암석과 동일한 방법으로 형태를 만들거나 가공할 수도 있으며(제2515호의 해설 참조), 또한 이 류의 주 제1호에 규정한 방법으로 가공하기도 한다. 비누석(soapstone)은 천연 동석(凍石)의 변종이다.\n\n이 호에 해당하는 활석(talc) : 이 류의 주 제1호에 규정한 방법으로 가공 처리하기도 하는데 석의 일반적인 형태는 가공하지 않은 모양이거나 가루 모양이다.\n\n“프렌치 초크(French chalk)”라는 용어는 가루 형태의 여러 종류의 동석(凍石)이나 활석을 가리킨다.\n\n이 호에는 동석(凍石)으로 만든 “재단사 초크(tailor’s chalk)”는 제외한다(제9609호)."
"이 호에는 동석(凍石)으로 만든 “재단사 초크(tailor’s chalk)”는 제외한다(제9609호).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 25.26]\n\n25.26 Natural steatite, whether or not roughly trimmed or merely cut, by sawing or otherwise, into blocks or slabs of a rectangular (including square) shape; talc.\n\n2526.10 Not crushed, not powdered\n\n2526.20 Crushed or powdered\n\nBoth natural steatite and talc are mineral substances rich in hydrous magnesium silicate. The former is more compact and massive than talc. Talc is foliated and softer and soapier to the touch.\n\nNatural steatite of this heading may be shaped or processed in the same ways as the stones of heading 25.15 (see the Explanatory Note to that heading) and may be submitted to the processes allowed by Note 1 to this Chapter. Soapstone is a variety of natural steatite.\n\nTalc of this heading may be submitted to the processes allowed by Note 1 to this Chapter. The more commonly met forms of talc are crude or powdered.\n\nThe term “French chalk” is used to designate certain varieties of steatite or talc in powder form.\n\nThe heading excludes “tailor’s chalks” which are composed of steatite (heading 96.09)."

Headings 26.01 to 26.17 do not, however, include minerals which have been submitted to processes not normal to the metallurgical industry.\n\n3.- Heading 26.20 applies only to :\n\n(a) Slag, ash and residues of a kind used in industry either for the extraction of metals or as a basis for the manufacture of chemical compounds of metals, excluding ash and residues from the incineration of municipal waste (heading 26.21); and\n\n(b) Slag, ash and residues containing arsenic, whether or not containing metals, of a kind used either for the extraction of arsenic or metals or for the manufacture of their chemical compounds.\n\n°\n\n° °\n\nSubheading Notes.\n\n1.- For the purposes of subheading 2620.21, “leaded gasoline sludges and leaded anti-knock compound sludges” mean sludge

"(e) Ores of the rare-earth metals of heading 25.30.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n26.01 - 철광과 그 정광(精鑛)[배소(焙燒)한 황화철광을 포함한다]\n\n- 철광과 그 정광[배소(焙燒)한 황화철광은 제외한다]\n\n2601.11 - 응결시키지 않은 것\n\n2601.12 - 응결시킨 것\n\n2601.20 - 배소(焙燒)한 황화철광\n\n이 호에 분류하는 주요 광(ore)은 다음과 같다.\n\n(a) 적(赤)철광(red haematite) : 경철광(specular iron ore)과 마르타이트(martite)(산화철)과 갈철광[brown haematite(minette)](철과 칼슘의 탄산염을 내포하고 있는 함수산화철)을 포함한다.\n\n(b) 갈철광(limonite) : 함수 산화철\n\n(c) 자(磁)철광(magnetite) : 자성(magnetic) 산화철\n\n(d) 능철석(siderite or chalybite) : 탄산철\n\n(e) 배소한 황화철광 또는 황철광의 배소재(응결한 것인지의 여부를 불문한다)\n\n이 호에는 또한 건조 중량으로 계산한 망간의 함유량이 20% 미만인 망간 철광과 그 정광(精鑛)[섭씨 105°C에서 110°C의 범위 내로 가열한 광(鑛)과 그 정광(精鑛)]을 포함하며(제2602호 해설 참조) ; 이들 광(鑛)은 망간의 함유량에 따라 망간 철광으로 알려지거나 철망간광으로 알려져 있다.\n\n이 호에는 안료용(pigment)으로 사용하기 위하여 곱게 가루로 한 자철광(magnetite)과 그 밖의 곱게 가루로 한 철광(iron ore)은 제외한다(제32류)."
"이 호에는 안료용(pigment)으로 사용하기 위하여 곱게 가루로 한 자철광(magnetite)과 그 밖의 곱게 가루로 한 철광(iron ore)은 제외한다(제32류).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 26.01]\n\n26.01 Iron ores and concentrates, including roasted iron pyrites.\n\nIron ores and concentrates, other than roasted iron pyrites :\n\n2601.11 Non-agglomerated\n\n2601.12 Agglomerated\n\n2601.20 Roasted iron pyrites\n\nThe principal ores generally classified in this heading are :\n\n(a) Red haematite, including specular iron ore and martite iron oxides and brown haematite (minettes) hydrated iron oxide containing iron and calcium carbonates.\n\n(b) Limonite, hydrated iron oxide.\n\n(c) Magnetite, magnetic iron oxide.\n\n(d) Siderite or chalybite, iron carbonate.\n\n(e) Roasted iron pyrites or pyrites cinders, whether or not agglomerated.\n\nThe heading also covers iron ores and concentrates with a manganese content of less than 20 % calculated on the dry weight (the ores and concentrates being heated to a temperature of 105 to 110 °C) (see Explanatory Note to heading 26.02). Depending upon their manganese content, these ores are known either as manganiferous iron ores or as ferruginous manganese ores.\n\nThe heading excludes finely ground magnetite and other finely ground iron ores for use as pigments (Chapter 32)."
"The heading excludes finely ground magnetite and other finely ground iron ores for use as pigments (Chapter 32).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n26.02 - 망간광과 그 정광(精鑛)[건조 상태에서 측정한 망간의 함유량이 전 중량의 100분의 20 이상인 철망간광과 그 정광(精鑛)을 포함한다]\n\n이 호에 보통 분류하는 주요 광(ore)은 다음과 같다.\n\n(a) 갈망간광(braunite) : 산화망간\n\n(b) 능망간광(rhodochrosite)(또는 dialogite) : 탄산망간\n\n(c) 하우스망광(hausmannite) : 함염 산화망간\n\n(d) 수망간광(manganite) : 함수 산화망간\n\n(e) 경망간광(psilomelane) : 함수 이산화망간\n\n(f) 연망간광(pyrolusite)(또는 polianite) : 이산화망간\n\n이 호에는 또한 철망간광과 그 정광(精鑛 : concentrate)을 포함하며, 건조 중량으로 계산한 망간의 함유량이 20% 이상의 것[섭씨 105°C에서 110°C의 범위 내로 가열한 광(鑛)과 그 정광(精鑛)]을 포함하며 ; 건조 중량으로 계산한 망간의 함유량이 20% 미만인 것은 제외한다(제2601호).\n\n이 호에는 또한 건전지 제조용으로 사용하는 연망간광(pyrolusite)은 제외한다(제2530호)."
"이 호에는 또한 건전지 제조용으로 사용하는 연망간광(pyrolusite)은 제외한다(제2530호).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 26.02]\n\n26.02 Manganese ores and concentrates, including ferruginous manganese ores and concentrates with a manganese content of 20 % or more, calculated on the dry weight.\n\nThe principal ores generally covered by this heading are :\n\n(a) Braunite, manganese oxide.\n\n(b) Rhodochrosite (or dialogite), manganese carbonate.\n\n(c) Hausmannite, saline manganese oxide.\n\n(d) Manganite, hydrated manganese oxide.\n\n(e) Psilomelane, hydrated manganese dioxide.\n\n(f) Pyrolusite (or polianite), manganese dioxide.\n\nThe heading also covers ferruginous manganese ores and concentrates, provided they have a manganese content of 20 % or more, calculated on the dry weight (the ores and concentrates being heated to a temperature of 105 to 110 °C); those with a manganese content of less than 20 % calculated on the dry weight are excluded (heading 26.01).\n\nThe heading also excludes pyrolusite prepared for use in dry batteries (heading 25.30).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n26.03 - 구리광과 그 정광\n\n이 호에 보통 분류하는 주요 광(ore)은 다음과 같다.\n\n(a) 녹염동광(atacamite) : 천연 히드록시 염화구리\n\n(b) 남동광(azurite) : 염기성 탄산동\n\n(c) 반동광(bornite나 erubescite) : 구리과 철의 황화물\n\n(d) 차골광(bournonite) : 구리ㆍ납과 안티모니의 황화물\n\n(e) 수등반(brochantite) : 염기성 황산구리\n\n(f) 휘동광(chalcocite) : 구리의 황화구리\n\n(g) 황동광(chalcopyrite)(copper pyrites) : 구리와 철의 황화물\n\n(h) 규공작석(chrysocolla) : 함수 규산구리\n\n(ij) 코벨라이트(covellite) : 황화구리\n\n(k) 적동광(cuprite) : 산화제일구리\n\n(l) 취동광(dioptase) : 규산구리\n\n(m) 회동광(grey copper ore)(간혹 은을 함유한다) : 구리와 안티모니의 황화물(tetrahedrit

"Indium, gallium, rhenium, hafnium, thallium and cadmium are not extracted directly from one particular ore, but are obtained as by products of the metallurgy of other metals (e.g., zinc, lead, copper, aluminium, zirconium, molybdenum).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n26.18 - 철강을 제조할 때 생기는 알갱이 모양의 슬래그(slag)[슬래그 샌드(slag sand)]\n\n이 호에는 용광로에서 액체 상태 드로스를 옮겨 물속에 부어 넣음으로써 얻는 알갱이 모양의 슬래그(slag)[슬래그 샌드(slag sand)]를 분류한다.\n\n한편 용융(溶融 : molten) 슬래그(slag)에 소량의 물을 가하여 만든 거품 형태의 슬래그(slag)와 용융(溶融) 슬래그에 압축 공기나 증기를 통하여 얻은 슬래그울(slag wool)은 제외한다(제6806호). 또한 이 호에는 슬래그 시멘트도 제외한다(제2523호)."
"한편 용융(융 : molten) 슬래그(slag)에 소량의 물을 가하여 만든 거품 형태의 슬래그(slag)와 용융(용융) 슬래그에 압축 공기나 증기를 통하여 얻은 슬래그울(slag wool)은 제외한다(제6806호). 또한 이 호에는 슬래그 시멘트도 제외한다(제2523호).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 26.18]\n\n26.18 Granulated slag (slag sand) from the manufacture of iron or steel.\n\nThis heading covers granulated slag (slag sand) obtained, for example, by pouring liquid dross into water as it leaves the blast furnace.\n\nOn the other hand, it does not include slag wool obtained by blowing steam or compressed air through molten slag, nor foamed slag made by adding small amounts of water to molten slag (heading 68.06). The heading also excludes slag cements (heading 25.23)."
"한편 용융(용융 : molten) 슬래그(slag)에 소량의 물을 가하여 만든 거품 형태의 슬래그(slag)와 용융(용융) 슬래그에 압축 공기나 증기를 통하여 얻은 슬래그울(slag wool)은 제외한다(제6806호). 또한 이 호에는 슬래그 시멘트도 제외한다(제2523호).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 26.18]\n\n26.18 Granulated slag (slag sand) from the manufacture of iron or steel.\n\nThis heading covers granulated slag (slag sand) obtained, for example, by pouring liquid dross into water as it leaves the blast furnace.\n\nOn the other hand, it does not include slag wool obtained by blowing steam or compressed air through molten slag, nor foamed slag made by adding small amounts of water to molten slag (heading 68.06). "On the other hand, it does not include slag wool obtained by blowing steam or compressed air through molten slag, nor foamed slag made by adding small amounts of water to molten slag (heading 68.06). The heading also excludes slag cements (heading 25.23).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n26.19 - 철강을 제조할 때 생기는 슬래그(slag)ㆍ드로스(dross)[알갱이 모양의 슬래그(slag)는 제외한다], 스케일링(scaling)과 그 밖의 웨이스트(waste)\n\n이 호에 분류하는 슬래그(slag)는 알루미늄ㆍ칼슘이나 철의 규산염으로 철광의 용해[고로(高爐)슬래그]ㆍ선철의 정제나 철강의 제조(전로 슬래그) 공정 중에서 얻는다. 이 호에는 금속을 회수할 수 있는 충분한 철분을 함유하고 있는지에 상관없이 이들 슬래그(slag)를 포함한다. 그러나 인산 슬래그[\"베이직 슬래그(basic slag)\"나 \"토마스 슬래그(Thomas slag)\"]는 제외하며 ; 중요한 비료로 제31류에 분류한다.\n\n슬래그(slag)와 드로스(dross)는 시멘트 제조ㆍ밸러스트(ballast)와 도로 건설에 사용한다. 머캐덤(macadam)과 같은 거칠게 부순 등급의 슬래그는 제2517호에 해당한다. 또한 이 호에는 알갱이 모양의 슬래그(slag)[슬래그 샌드(slag sand)]는 제외한다(제2618호).\n\n스케일링(scaling)은 산화철의 칩(chip)으로서 철강의 단조ㆍ열간압연 등으로부터 발생한다.\n\n이 호에는 용광로에서 생긴 더스트(dust)와 철강을 제조할 때에 생긴 그 밖의 종류의 웨이스트(waste)를 포함한다. 그러나 절단ㆍ조형이나 그 밖의 금속가공 공정에서 생긴 금속 스크랩(scrap)은 제7204호에 해당한다."
"이 호에는 용광로에서 생긴 더스트(dust)와 철강을 제조할 때에 생긴 그 밖의 종류의 웨이스트(waste)를 포함한다. 그러나 절단ㆍ조형이나 그 밖의 금속가공 공정에서 생긴 금속 스크랩(scrap)은 제7204호에 해당한다.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 26.19]\n\n26.19 Slag, dross (other than granulated slag), scalings and other waste from the manufacture of iron or steel.\n\nThe slags covered by this heading are silicates of aluminium, calcium or iron obtained during the smelting of iron ore (blast furnace slag), the refining of pig iron or the manufacture of steel (converter slag). The heading includes these slags whether or not they contain sufficient iron to permit the recovery of the metal. But it excludes the phosphatic slags (“basic slag” or “Thomas slag”); these are important fertilisers and are classified in Chapter 31.\n\nSlag and dross are used in the manufacture of cement, for ballast and in road construction. Slag crushed and roughly graded as macadam falls in heading 25.17. The heading also excludes granulated slag (slag sand) of heading 26.18.\n\nScalings are chips of iron oxide which result from the forging, hot rolling, etc., of iron or steel.\n\nThe heading also includes dust from blast furnaces and other kinds of waste resulting from the manufacture of iron and steel, but not scrap metal produced during cutting, shaping or other metal working processes, which falls in heading 72.04."

Such products are generally used as potting soils.\n\nHowever, the heading does not cover :\n\n(a) Fibres of peat (known as “berandine”) prepared for textile use (Section XI).\n\n(b) Flower pots or other articles of peat, including insulating sheets for buildings, obtained by cutting or moulding (Chapter 68)."

Such products are generally used as potting soils.\n\nHowever, the heading does not cover :\n\n(a) Fibres of peat (known as “berandine”) prepared for textile use (Section XI).\n\n(b) Flower pots or other articles of peat, including insulating sheets for buildings, obtained by cutting or moulding (Chapter 68)."
"(b) Flower pots or other articles of peat, including insulating sheets for buildings, obtained by cutting or moulding (Chapter 68).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n27.04 - 코크스와 반성(半成) 코크스(석탄ㆍ갈탄ㆍ토탄으로 제조한 것으로 한정하며, 응결시킨 것인지에 상관없다), 레토르트 카본(retort carbon)\n\n코크스(coke)는 석탄ㆍ갈탄ㆍ토탄을 공기와 차단하여 증류(또는 탄화하거나 가스화)하여 얻은 고체 형태의 잔유물이다. 이것은 코크스 노(爐) 내의 여러 가지 품질의 유연탄에서 얻어진다.\n\n반성(半成) 코크스(semi-coke)는 석탄이나 갈탄을 저온에서 증류하여 얻는다.\n\n이 호의 코크스와 반성 코크스는 잘게 부수거나 응결시키는 경우도 있다.\n\n레토르트 카본(retort carbon)(가스 탄소)은 단단한 흑색으로 부서지기 쉬운 탄소 형태이며, 두드리면 금속성 소리가 난다. 이는 가스공업에서나 코크스 노(爐)에서 노(爐)나 레토르트의 벽에 부착되는 경우 그 부산물로 얻어진다. 이 탄소는 보통 불규칙한 덩어리로 구성되는데 레토르트의 모양에 따라 그 면이 평면이거나 약간 굴곡이 있다.\n\n일부 국가에서는 레토르트 카본(retort carbon)을 “인조 흑연(artificial graphite)”이라고도 하나 이 명칭은 제3801호에 해당하는 인공 생산된 흑연에 적용하는 것이 보다 정확하다.\n\n이 호에서는 다음의 물품을 제외한다.\n\n(a) 피치코크스와 석유코크스(각각 제2708호와 제2713호)\n\n(b) 전기(電氣)용으로 사용하는 종류의 레토르트의 카본(retort carbon) 제품(제8545호)"
"(b) 전기(電氣)용으로 사용하는 종류의 레토르트의 카본(retort carbon) 제품(제8545호)\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 27.04]\n\n27.04 Coke and semi coke of coal, of lignite or of peat, whether or not agglomerated; retort carbon.\n\nCoke is the solid residue obtained from the distillation (or carbonisation or gasification) of coal, lignite or peat in the absence of air. It is obtained in coke ovens from various qualities of bituminous coals.\n\nSemi coke results from the distillation of coal or lignite at low temperature.\n\nCoke and semi coke of this heading may be pulverised or agglomerated.\n\nRetort carbon (gas carbon) is a hard, black, brittle form of carbon which gives a metallic ring when struck. It is obtained as a by product in gas works or coke ovens where it is deposited on the walls of the ovens or retorts. The carbon usually consists of irregular lumps of which one face is either flat or slightly curved according to the shape of the retort.\n\nIn some countries, retort carbon is called “artificial graphite”, but this name is more correctly applied to artificially produced graphite of heading 38.01.\n\nThe heading excludes :\n\n(a) Pitch coke and petroleum coke (headings 27.08 and 27.13, respectively).\n\n(b) Articles of retort carbon of a kind used for electrical purposes (heading 85.45)."
"(b) Articles of retort carbon of a kind used for electrical purposes (heading 85.45).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n27.05 - 석탄가스ㆍ수성(水性)가스ㆍ발생로(發生爐)가스와 이와 유사한 가스(석유가스와 그 밖의 가스 상태의 탄화수소는 제외한다)\n\n석탄 가스(coal gas)는 보통 가스 공업이나 코크스노(爐 : oven)에서 공기를 차단하여 석탄을 증류하여 얻는다. 이는 수소ㆍ메탄(methane)ㆍ일산화탄소 등의 복합 혼합물로 빛이나 열을 내는데 사용한다.\n\n이 호는 지하가스화법(underground gasification)에서 얻은 가스와 수성(水性)가스ㆍ발생로 가스(發生爐 가스 : producer gas)와 이와 유사한 가스(예: 용광로 가스)를 포함한다. 또한 보통 수증기 존재 하에서 광물유(鑛物油 : mineral oil)ㆍ석유가스나 천연가스를 분해하거나 개량하여 생긴 혼합 가스도 분류한다. 이들 혼합 가스는 석탄가스와 조성이 유사하며, 열이나 빛을 내는데 사용하며, 화학물질(예: 메탄올ㆍ암모니아) 합성에 사용한다(후자의 경우에 이들은 때로 합성가스라고 불리기도 한다). 그러나 이 호에서는 제2711호에 열거한 가스를 제외한다."

It is a complex mixture of hydrogen, methane, carbon monoxide, etc., and is used for lighting or heating purposes.\n\nThe heading includes the gas produced by underground gasification and also water gas, producer gas and similar gases, for example blast furnace gas. It also covers mixtures of gases formed by cracking or reforming of mineral oils, petroleum gases or natural gases, usually in the presence of steam. These mixtures are similar in composition to coal gas and are used for heating or lighting purposes and in the synthesis of chemicals, e.g., methanol, ammonia. In the latter case, they are sometimes called “synthesis gas”. However, the heading excludes the gases specified in heading 27.11."

"Pitch coke is the final residual product left from the distillation of either high temperature or low temperature coal tars or of other mineral tars or of their pitches. "This subheading covers sedimentary rock or sand containing hydrocarbons, which can be separated in the form of products of heading 27.09 (Petroleum oils and oils obtained from bituminous minerals, crude), or in a form from which these products can be extracted. Gas and other products may also be obtained. The separation is achieved by heating or other extraction processes (e.g., by distillation, retorting or mechanical processes). The hydrocarbons contained in shale may be in the form of organic materials called kerogens.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n27.15 - 역청질(瀝靑質) 혼합물[천연 아스팔트, 천연 역청(瀝靑), 석유역청(瀝靑), 광물성 타르, 광물성 타르 피치(tar pitch)를 기본 재료로 한 것으로 한정한다(예: 역청질 매스틱과 컷백)]\n\n이 호에 분류하는 역청질(瀝靑質) 혼합물은 다음과 같다.\n\n(1) 컷백(cut-back)은 일반적으로 60% 이상의 아스팔트에 용제가 함유된 것이며 도로 포장용에 사용한다.\n\n(2) 아스팔트ㆍ피치(pitch)나 타르를 유화(乳化 : emulsion)하거나 물에서 안정분산시킨 것이며 특히 도로포장용으로 사용하는 종류의 것\n\n(3) 아스팔트 매스틱(mastic)과 그 밖의 역청질(瀝靑質)의 매스틱ㆍ모래나 석면과 같은 광물성 물질과 혼합한 역청질(瀝靑質)의 혼합물. 이들 물품은 충전 재료나 성형 재료 등에 사용한다.\n\n이 호에는 사용 전에 재용융(再溶融 : re-melted)하는 종류의 것으로서 블록 등으로 응결된 물품까지 포함되며 규칙적인 형태의 최종 제품[도로포장용 판석(paving flagstone)ㆍ판(板)과 타일과 같은 것]은 제외한다(제6807호).\n\n이 호에는 다음의 물품은 제외한다.\n\n(a) 타르 머캐덤(tarred macadam)(타르와 혼합하여 부순 돌)(제2517호)\n\n(b) 타르와 응집한 백운석(白雲石)(제2518호)\n\n(c) 피치(pitch)에 크레오소트유(creosote oil)나 그 밖의 콜타르(coal tar

"Except where the context otherwise requires, double or complex inorganic salts are to be classified in heading 28.42 (see Note 5 to Chapter 28 and Explanatory Note to heading 28.42).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n제1절\n\n원소\n\n(제2801호 - 제2805호)\n\n총설\n\n원소는 비(非)금속과 금속의 두 종류로 분류할 수 있다. 이 절에는 적어도 어떤 형태이든 간에 모든 비(非)금속이 포함되지만 반면에 다수의 금속은 다른 호에 분류한다[예: 귀금속(제71류와 제2843호)․비금속(卑金屬 : base metal)(제72류에서 제76류까지와 제78류에서 제81류까지)과 방사성 원소와 동위원소(제2844호)와 안정동위원소(제2845호)].\n\n적당한 분류를 표시하기 위하여 알려진 여러 원소의 일람표를 별표와 같이 제시하였다. 안티모니(antimony)와 같이 특정 원소는 금속과 비(非)금속의 두 가지 역할을 한다. 이 같은 점이 이 표 해설서 상의 분류에서 주의하여야 할 점이다.\n\n/clip/namoedit/2021/12/31/wco/haesul2022/kor/t03/kor_t03_hs2801_01.jpg\n\n/clip/namoedit/2021/12/31/wco/haesul2022/kor/t03/kor_t03_hs2801_02.jpg\n\n28.01 - 플루오르ㆍ염소ㆍ브롬ㆍ요드\n\n2801.10 - 염소\n\n2801.20 - 요드\n\n2801.30 - 플루오르와 브롬\n\n이 호에는 할로겐 원소로서 알려진 비(非)금속을 분류한다[아스타틴(At)은 제외한다(제2844호)].\n\n(A) 플루오르(fluorine)\n\n플루오르(fluorine)는 자극성 냄새를 가진 희미한 녹황색 가스이며 ; 점막을 자극하므로 흡입하면 위험하며 부식성(腐蝕性)이 있다. 이는 철제 용기에 가압하여 제시하며 ; 특히 목재ㆍ지방(fat)과 직물(textile)과 같은 유기 물질을 발화시키는 매우 활성 있는 원소이다.\n\n플루오르는 특정 플루오르화물과 유기플루오르 유도체의 제조용으로 사용한다.\n\n(B) 염소(chlorine)\n\n염소(chlorine)는 보통 염화알칼리 특히 염화나트륨

"The heading excludes unrefined sulphur as obtained by the Frasch process and refined sulphur, even though they are often very pure (heading 25.03).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n28.03 - 탄소[카본 블랙(carbon black)과 따로 분류되지 않은 탄소물품을 포함한다]\n\n탄소는 고체 상태의 비(非)금속 원소이다.\n\n이 호에는 다음 범주의 탄소를 포함한다.\n\n카본 블랙(carbon black)은 카본을 많이 함유하고 있는 다음과 같은 유기물을 불완전연소하거나 크래킹(cracking)(열ㆍ전기 아크나 전기 스파크)하여 생긴다.\n\n(1) 메탄(methane)ㆍ안트라센(anthracenic)가스[즉, 안트라센(anthracene)을 탄화시켜 생긴 가스]와 아세틸렌과 같은 천연 가스ㆍ아세틸렌 블랙은 극히 미세하고 순수한 물품으로 압축 아세틸렌을 전기 스파크로 급분해하여 얻어진다.\n\n(2) 나프탈렌․수지․오일(oil)(램프 블랙)\n\n카본 블랙은 제조방법에 따라 채널 블랙(channel black)이나 퍼니스 블랙(furnace black)으로 불리기도 한다.\n\n카본 블랙은 오일 상태의 불순물을 함유하기도 한다.\n\n카본 블랙은 페인트ㆍ인쇄용 잉크ㆍ구두광택제(shoe-polish) 등의 제조용 안료(顔料 : pigment)로 사용하며, 카본지의 제조ㆍ고무 공업에서 보강제로 사용한다.\n\n이 호에는 다음의 것을 제외한다.\n\n(a) 천연 흑연(natural graphite)(제2504호)\n\n(b) 고체연료 모양의 천연 탄소(무연탄ㆍ석탄ㆍ갈탄) ; 코크스(coke)ㆍ응결연료와 가스탄소(제27류)\n\n(c) 제3206호의 특정 광물성 착색제인 블랙[예: 알루블랙(alu black)ㆍ셰일블랙(shale black)ㆍ실리카블랙(silica black)]\n\n(d) 인조 흑연(graphite) ; 콜로이드 상태의 흑연과 반(반)콜로이드(semi-colloidal) 상태의 흑연(예: 제3801호)\n\n(e) 활성탄과 애니멀 블랙(제3802호)\n\n(f) 목탄(wood charcoal)(제4402호)\n\n(h) 다이아몬드형의 결정

"Some of the non metals in this group (for example, silicon and selenium) may be doped with elements such as boron, phosphorus, etc., in a proportion generally of the order of one part per million, with a view to their use in electronics. They are classified in this heading provided that they are in forms unworked as drawn, or in the form of cylinders or rods. "Mercury is used for preparing the amalgams of heading 28.43 or 28.53. It is used in gold or silver metallurgy, in the gold or silver plating industries, and in the manufacture of chlorine, sodium hydroxide, mercury salts, vermilion or fulminates. It is also used for making mercury vapour lamps and in various physical instruments, in medicine, etc.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n제2절\n\n무기산과 무기 비(非)금속 산화물\n\n(제2806호 - 제2811호)\n\n총설\n\n산(acid)은 금속[또는 유사한 성질을 가진 이온(ion), 예를 들면, 암모늄(NH4+)]으로 일부나 전부를 치환할 수 있는 수소를 함유하고 있으며, 치환하면 염을 생성한다. 산은 염기와 반응하여 염을 생성하며 알코올과 반응하여 에스테르(ester)를 생성한다. 액체 상태나 용액에서는 산은 전기 분해되어 음극에서 수소가 발생한다. 산소를 함유하고 있는 산에서 1분자 이상의 물을 제거할 경우는 산무수물을 얻으며 대부분의 비(非)금속 산화물은 산무수물이다.\n\n이 절에는 비(非)금속의 무기 산소화합물[무수물(無水物 : anhydride)과 그 밖의 것]ㆍ무기산[양극기가 비(非)금속으로 되어 있는 것]도 분류한다.\n\n한편 이 호에는 금속산화물이나 금속수산화물로 각각 생성된 산과 산무수물은 제외하며 ; 이들은 일반적으로 제4절에 해당한다[예: 크로뮴(chromium)ㆍ몰리브데늄(molybdenum)ㆍ텅스텐(tungsten)과 바나듐(vanadium)의 산과 산무수물과 같은 금속의 산화물ㆍ수산화물과 과산화물]. 그러나 특정의 경우에 있어서는 다른 호에 해당한다[예: 제2843호(귀금속의 화합물)ㆍ제2844호나 제2845호(방사성 원소와 동위원소)ㆍ제2846호[희토류(稀토류)금속ㆍ스칸듐이나 이트륨의 화합물].\n\n또한 수소의 산소화합물은 제외하며 제2201호(물)ㆍ제2845호[중수(

"(b) 인화수소(제2853호)\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 28.09]\n\n28.09 Diphosphorus pentaoxide; phosphoric acid; polyphosphoric acids, whether or not chemically defined.\n\n2809.10 Diphosphorus pentaoxide\n\n2809.20 Phosphoric acid and polyphosphoric acids\n\nThis heading covers diphosphorus pentaoxide, phosphoric acid (orthophosphoric acid or common phosphoric acid), pyrophosphoric (diphosphoric) acid, metaphosphoric and other polyphosphoric acids.\n\n(A) DIPHOSPHORUS PENTAOXIDE\n\nDiphosphorus pentaoxide (phosphorus (V) oxide, phosphorus pentoxide, phosphoric anhydride) (P2O5) is obtained by combustion, in dry air, of phosphorus extracted from natural phosphates. It is a very corrosive white powder, absorbs water avidly, and is transported in airtight packings. It is used for drying gases, and in organic synthesis.\n\nDiphosphorus pentaoxide exists in crystalline, amorphous or vitreous form. These three varieties mixed together give “phosphoric snow”, classified here.\n\n(B) PHOSPHORIC ACID\n\nPhosphoric acid (orthophosphoric acid or common phosphoric acid) (H3PO4) is obtained by the action of sulphuric acid on natural tricalcium phosphate. The commercial acid thus prepared contains as impurities diphosphorus pentaoxide, calcium dihydrogenorthophosphate, sulphur trioxide, sulphuric acid, fluorosilicic acid, etc. Pure phosphoric acid is obtained by controlled hydration of diphosphorus pentaoxide.\n\nPhosphoric acid may be in the form of deliquescent prismatic crystals, but, as it is difficult to preserve in the solid state, it is usually put up in aqueous solutions (e.g., 65 %, 90 %). The concentrated solution, which remains supersaturated at room temperature, is sometimes known as “syrupy phosphoric acid”.\n\nPhosphoric acid is used to prepare concentrated (triple) superphosphates; it is also employed in the textile industries and as a pickling (rust removing) agent.\n\nPhosphoric acid by condensation at high temperature gives rise to several polymeric

"안티모니(antimony)는 이 표에서 금속으로 취급하므로 안티모니산과 산화안티모니는 제2825호에 해당한다.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 28.11]\n\n28.11 Other inorganic acids and other inorganic oxygen compounds of non-metals.\n\nOther inorganic acids :\n\n2811.11 Hydrogen fluoride (hydrofluoric acid)\n\n2811.12 Hydrogen cyanide (hydrocyanic acid)\n\n2811.19 Other\n\nOther inorganic oxygen compounds of non metals :\n\n2811.21 Carbon dioxide\n\n2811.22 Silicon dioxide\n\n2811.29 Other\n\nThis heading covers mineral acids and anhydrides and other oxides of non metals. The most important are listed below according to their non metal component () :\n\nFootnotes.\n\n() In the following order : fluorine, chlorine, bromine, iodine, sulphur, selenium, tellurium, nitrogen, phosphorus, arsenic, carbon, silicon.\n\n(A) COMPOUNDS OF FLUORINE\n\n(1) Hydrogen fluoride (HF). Obtained by the action of sulphuric acid on natural calcium fluoride (fluorite) or on cryolite. It is purified by treatment with potassium carbonate or by distillation (it sometimes contains small quantities of silicates and of fluorosilicic acid as impurities). In the anhydrous state, hydrogen fluoride is an extremely hygroscopic liquid (boiling point 18/20 °C); it fumes in a humid atmosphere. In the anhydrous state and in concentrated solution (hydrofluoric acid) it burns deeply into the skin and carbonises organic matter. It is presented in metal bottles lined with lead, gutta percha or ceresine wax, or in rubber or plastic containers; the very pure acid is kept in silver flasks.\n\nIts uses include etching on glass, manufacturing ashless filter paper, preparation of tantalum, of fluorides, scouring and pickling foundry pieces, in organic synthesis, or as a control in fermenting processes.\n\n(2) Fluoroacids. "과산화칼륨(potassium peroxide : dipotassium dioxide)(K2O2)은 제조공정ㆍ성질과 용도에 대해 과산화나트륨과 매우 유사하다.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - Sub-Chapter IV]\n\nSub-Chapter IV\n\nINORGANIC BASES AND OXIDES, HYDROXIDES\n\nAND PEROXIDES OF METAL\n\nGENERAL\n\nBases are compounds characterised by a hydroxyl radical (OH) and which react with acids to form salts. In the liquid state or in solution, they are electrolytes giving a metal or an analogous ion (ammonium (NH4+)) at the cathode.\n\nMetal oxides are compounds of a metal with oxygen. Many can combine with one or more molecules of water to form hydroxides.\n\nMost oxides are basic since their hydroxides act as bases. Certain oxides (anhydride oxides), however, react only with alkaline or other bases to form salts, while another more common class (amphoteric oxides) can behave both as anhydride oxides or as bases. These classes of oxides must be regarded as anhydrides of acids, real or hypothetical, corresponding to their hydroxides.\n\nCertain oxides (saline oxides) may be regarded as resulting from the combination of a basic oxide with an anhydride oxide.\n\nThis sub Chapter covers :\n\n(1) Oxides, hydroxides and peroxides of metal, whether basic, acidic, amphoteric or saline.\n\n(2) Other inorganic bases containing no oxygen, such as ammonia (heading 28.14), or hydrazine (heading 28.25), and those containing no metal, such as hydroxylamine (heading 28.25).\n\nThe sub Chapter excludes :\n\n(a) The oxides and hydroxides of Chapter 25, particularly magnesia (magnesium oxide), whether or not pure, and quicklime and slaked lime (crude calcium oxide and hydroxide).\n\n(b) Oxides and hydroxides constituting ores (headings 26.01 to 26.17), scalings, ash, slag, dross, scum or other metalliferous residues (headings 26.18 to 26.20).\n\n(c) Oxides, peroxides and hydroxides of precious metals (heading 28.43), of radioactive elements (heading 28.44), of rare-earth metals, of yttrium or of
<truncated 6216 bytes>

White powder, heavier than magnesium oxide; stable but forming the carbonate slowly when exposed to air. Used in pharmacy.\n\n(2) Magnesium peroxide (MgO2). Prepared by the action of hydrogen peroxide on magnesium hydroxide. White powder, containing oxide as impurity; almost insoluble in water. Used for bleaching feathers, in preparing dentifrices or as a gastro intestinal antiseptic.\n\nMagnesium oxide is excluded (heading 25.19 or if in the form of cultured crystals weighing not less than 2.5 g each, heading 38.24).\n\n(B) STRONTIUM OXIDE, HYDROXIDE AND PEROXIDE\n\n(1) Strontium oxide (anhydrous or caustic strontia) (SrO). Prepared by calcining precipitated strontium carbonate. Porous white, hygroscopic powder, soluble in water. Forms the carbonate when exposed to air. Used in pyrotechnics or medicine and for preparing strontium hydroxide and pigments.\n\n(2) Strontium hydroxide (Sr(OH)2). Exists in the anhydrous amorphous state or crystallised with 8 H2O; forms the carbonate when exposed to air. Used in glass manufacture, and for the preparation of strontium salts and luminous pigments.\n\n(3) Strontium peroxide (SrO2). Prepared by the action of oxygen on strontium oxide. White powder, decomposed by hot water. Used in pyrotechnics.\n\n(C) BARIUM OXIDE, HYDROXIDE AND PEROXIDE\n\n(1) Barium oxide (anhydrous baryta) (BaO). This product must not be confused with natu

"(d) 불순한 산화아연(때로는 징크 그레이(zinc grey)로 알려진 것)(제3206호)\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 28.17]\n\n28.17 Zinc oxide; zinc peroxide.\n\n(A) ZINC OXIDE\n\nZinc oxide (zinc white) (ZnO) is obtained by burning zinc vapour with oxygen from air. The zinc vapour is obtained by vaporising metallic zinc (indirect or French process) or by the reduction of oxidic zinc raw materials like zinc ores (roasted blende, calamine –heading 26.08) with carbon (direct or American process). In these processes, the oxide is collected in bag houses or chambers forming deposits of increasingly pure oxides.\n\nIn the wet process, zinc is leached from zinc containing raw materials and then precipitated as zinc hydroxide or carbonate. The precipitate is filtered, washed, dried and calcinated to ZnO. Zinc oxide is a fine white powder which turns yellow on heating. It is of amphoteric nature, soluble in acids and alkalis.\n\nZinc oxide is mainly used in industrial paints. It is also used in the rubber industry, ceramic, glass manufacturing, electronics and pharmaceuticals. Zinc oxide is also a precursor of a wide variety of inorganic or organic salts used in the manufacture of plastics.\n\nThe zincates of heading 28.41 correspond to this amphoteric oxide.\n\n(B) ZINC PEROXIDE\n\nZinc peroxide (ZnO2) White powder, insoluble in water. Used in medicine, either pure or with zinc oxide as impurity, and also for preparing cosmetics.\n\nThis heading does not include :\n\n(a) Natural zinc oxide or zincite (heading 26.08).\n\n(b) Residues of zinc metallurgy known as zinc scurf, skimmings or dross, which also consist of impure oxides (heading 26.20).\n\n(c) Zinc hydroxide (Zn(OH)2) or gelatinous white, or the hydroperoxide (heading 28.25).\n\n(d) The impure zinc oxide, sometimes known as zinc grey (heading 32.06)."

The most important manganese oxide. Prepared by the action of a slightly nitric solution of potassium permanganate on a manganous salt (e.g., the sulphate). Brown or blackish masses or powder (specific gravity about 5), insoluble in water.\n\nA very powerful oxidising agent. Uses include pyrotechnics, organic synthesis (preparation of hydroxyanthraquinones, aminoanthraquinones, etc.), in gas masks, as a depolarising agent in batteries, in the ceramics industry, in the manufacture of driers, printers’ ink (manganese black), colours (brown pigments known as mineral bistre, manganese bitumen), certain mastics, and synthetic semi precious stones (artificial garnet). It is also used in the glass industry (glassmakers’ soap) generally to correct the yellow tint of glass.\n\nThis oxide has the character of an anhydride from which the manganites of heading 28.41 are derived.\n\nThis heading does not include the anhydrous natural manganese dioxide (pyrolusite) and hydrated natural manganese dioxide (psilomelane) (heading 26.02).\n\n(2) Manganese oxide (MnO). Greyish or greenish powder, insoluble in water. Specific gravity about 5.1. Used in textile printing.\n\nManganous hydroxide is excluded (heading 28.25).\n\n(3) Dimanganese trioxide (manganese sesquioxide, manganic oxide) (Mn2O3). This oxide is basic. Brown or black powder (specific gravity about 4.8), insoluble in water. Uses include : in textile printing, as a ceramic colour, in the glass industry, the manufacture of driers (manganese linoleate), as a catalyst in chemistry, inorganic (manufacture of nitric acid) or organic.\n\nThe heading does not include natural manganic oxide (braunite heading 26.02), nor manganic hydroxide (heading 28.25).\n\n(4) Manganomanganic oxide (manganese saline oxide) (Mn3O4). For the purposes of assessing whether the 70 % limit has been reached, account must be taken of the total iron content expressed as ferric oxide; thus a natural ferrous earth colour containing 84 % of ferric oxide (representing 58.8 % of pure iron) remains classified in the heading.\n\nThe heading also includes the following artificial oxides and hydroxides :\n\n(A) IRON OXIDES\n\nFerric oxide (Fe2O3). It is used for making ordinary or anti rust paints, compounds for burnishing metal or polishing glass, and vitrifiable compounds used to render the mass fusible in the manufacture of bottle glass. A grey, brown or greenish powder.\n\n(2) Dicobalt trioxide (cobalt sesquioxide, cobaltic oxide) (Co2O3). Black powder.\n\n(3) Tricobalt tetraoxide (cobalt saline oxide) (Co3O4). Black powder.\n\n(4) Commercial cobalt oxides. Generally greyish or black powder consisting of cobalt monoxide and cobalt saline oxide in various ratios.\n\nThese products serve in enamel works for preparing brilliant blue colours, and in the glass industry for colouring optical glass. They are converted into silicates (e.g., cobalt potassium silicates) for the manufacture of the vitrifiable colours of heading 32.07; these compounds are known as smalt, opaque glass, azure, enamel blue and Sèvres blue. The term “smalt” is applied indiscriminately to the oxides and to their silicates, both being obtained from a natural cobalt arsenide, smaltite, an ore classified in heading 26.05. A certain number of blue, green and violet artists’ paints are composed of cobalt oxides, aluminates, zincates and phosphates (sky blue, cerulean blue, cobalt green, cobalt violet).\n\nThe heading excludes crude cobalt oxides obtained from the treatment of argentiferous ores (heading 26.20).\n\n(B) COBALT HYDROXIDES\n\nThe term “cobalt hydroxide” covers not only cobaltous hydroxide (Co(OH)2), used for the preparation of driers, and cobaltic hydroxide (e.g., Co(OH)3), obtained in cobalt metallurgy, but also saline oxide hydrates. Lead or cerussite (lead hydrocarbonate) oxidised by heating in air produces first unmelted lead oxide or massicot, in the form of a pale yellow powder, and then, when the temperature passes the blood red heat point, the fused oxide, in orange yellow or reddish powder or scales. The term “litharge” covers both these products, but is applied more particularly to the latter. They are also obtained as by products of the extraction of silver from argentiferous lead. Lead oxide is used in the glass industry (manufacture of lead and crystal glass), in the enamel industry, and in the manufacture of matches, colours, driers, etc.\n\n(2) Trilead tetraoxide (Lead saline oxide, red lead, minium) (approximate formula Pb3O4). Obtained from unmelted lead monoxide (massicot). A toxic, orange red powder (specific gravity 8 to 9). The term orange lead is applied either to a very pure saline oxide, more highly coloured and less dense than the common variety, or to lead oxides still containing lead carbonate from the cerussite used in their preparation. Red lead is used for extending other colours (Saturn red), for preparing anti rust paints or mastics and for colouring sealing wax. It is also used as a pottery glaze. It is employed in the manufacture of crystal glass and optical glass even more widely than the monoxide, because it gives a fusible glass of remarkable brilliance arising from a high refractive index.\n\n(3) Lead dioxide (puce oxide, plumbic anhydride) (PbO2). Prepared by treating the lead saline oxide with nitric acid or by electrolysing lead nitrate. A brown powder, insoluble in water, capable of igniting organic matter on contact. "(d) Bismuth sulphide (bismuthinite) (heading 26.17).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n28.31 - 아이티온산염과 술폭실산염\n\n2831.10 - 아이티온산나트륨과 술폭실산나트륨\n\n2831.90 - 기타\n\n아이티온산염(dithionite) : 아이티온(dithionous)(“hydrosulphurous”)산의 염(H2S2O4)으로 유리(遊離)상태로 분리되지 않는다. 이산화황으로 포화시킨 아황산수소의 용액을 환원(아연 가루로)시켜 얻으며 화학 공업ㆍ직물 공업이나 제당 공업에서 주로 표백의 환원제로 사용한다.\n\n가장 중요한 것은 아이티온산나트륨(sodium dithionite)(Na2S2O4)으로 무수염은 백색 가루로서 물에 녹고 무색의 결정의 함수염(2H2O)을 가진 것이다. 유기 합성ㆍ염색공업ㆍ제지공업에 사용한다. 결정화된 상태에서도 급속도로 변질된다. 특정 용도(직물공업에 있어서 환원제)에서는 아이티온산나트륨을 포름알데히드로, 때로는 산화아연이나 글리세롤을 가하여 안정화시켜야 한다. 또한 아세톤으로 안정화시킬 수도 있다.\n\n아이티온산칼륨ㆍ아이티온산칼슘ㆍ아이티온산마그네슘ㆍ아이티온산아연 : 아이티온산나트륨과 유사한 물품으로서, 유사한 방법으로 안정화시킬 수 있으며 성질과 용도도 유사하다.\n\n이 호에는 모든 안정화시킨 아이티온산염과 유사물품인 술폭실산 포름알데히드(formaldehyde sulphoxylate)를 분류한다.\n\n아황산염과 티오황산염은 제외한다(제2832호).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 28.31]\n\n28.31 Dithionites and sulphoxylates.\n\n2831.10 Of sodium\n\n2831.90 Other\n\nDithionites (hydrosulphites) are the salts of dithionous (“hydrosulphurous”) acid (H2S2O4) which has not been isolated in the free state. "Sulphites and thiosulphates are excluded (heading 28.32).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n28.32 - 아황산염과 티오황산염\n\n2832.10 - 아황산나트륨\n\n2832.20 - 그 밖의 아황산염\n\n2832.30 - 티오황산염\n\n이 호에는 이 절의 서문에서 설명한 제외규정에 따라 다음의 것을 분류한다.\n\n(A) 금속의 아황산염(sulphite) : 아황산(H2SO3)의 염[수용액(水溶液) 상태만이 존재하며 제2811호의 이산화황에 상당하다]\n\n(B) 금속의 티오황산염(thiosulphate) : 티오황산(H2S2O3)의 염으로 순수 상태로 존재하지 않는다.\n\n이 호에는 아황산펄프 폐액(lye)의 농축물(제3804호)과 유기물로 안정화시킨 “히드로술파이트(hydrosulphite)”로 칭하는 공업용 물품(제2831호)은 제외한다.\n\n(A) 아황산염(sulphite)\n\n이 호에는 중성 아황산염 뿐만 아니라 산성 아황산염을 분류한다.\n\n(1) 아황산나트륨 : 아황산수소나트륨(NaHSO3)․이아황산이나트륨(Na2SO3SO2이나 Na2S2O5)과 아황산나트륨(Na2SO3)을 포함한다.\n\n(a) 아황산수소나트륨(sodium hydrogen sulphite) : 탄산나트륨의 수용액(水溶液)에 이산화황을 반응시켜 얻으며 무색 가루이나 결정으로서 비교적 불안정하며 이산화황의 냄새를 갖고 있으며 물에 매우 잘 녹는다. ; 또한 황색의 농축액으로 제시한다. 유기 합성의 환원제ㆍ인디고 제조ㆍ모(wool)나 견(silk)의 표백과 라텍스의 처리에 가황제ㆍ가죽 제조ㆍ포도 재배(와인의 방부제)와 부유선광(浮遊選鑛 : flotation)제에 사용한다.\n\n(b) 이황산이나트륨(sodium metabisulphite, pyrosulphite, dry sulphite)(특정 언어권에서는 “sodium bisulphite crystal”이라고 잘못 부르기도 한다) : 아황산수소에서 얻으며 ; 특히 습한 공기 중에서는 비교적 급히 산화한다. 용도는 산성 아황산염과 동일하며 포도 재배와 사진용에 사용한다.\

The most important is the tetraborate (disodium tetraborate, refined borax) (Na2B4O7). Obtained by crystallisation of solutions of natural borates, or by treating natural calcium borates or boric acid with sodium carbonate. Anhydrous or hydrated with 5 H2O or 10 H2O. Heated and then cooled down, it gives a vitreous mass (melted borax, borax glass, borax bead). Used for stiffening linen or paper; in soldering metals (flux for hard solder); as a flux for enamels; in the manufacture of vitrifiable colours, special glass (optical glass, glass for electric bulbs), glue or polish; for refining gold; and for preparing borates and anthraquinone dyes.\n\nThere are other sodium borates (metaborates, hydrogen diborate) for laboratory uses.\n\n(2) Ammonium borates. The most important of these is metaborate (NH4BO2.2H2O). Colourless crystals, very soluble in water, efflorescent. Decomposed by heat to give a fusible varnish of boric anhydride; hence its use as a fire proofing material. Also used as a fixative in hair lotions; as a component of electrolytes for electrolytic capacitors and in the coating of paper.\n\n(3) Precipita

This is separated from normal hydrogen in which it occurs at a level of 1 part in 6,500.\n\n(2) Heavy water, which is deuterium oxide. Occurring in ordinary water at a level of 1 part in 6,500. It is normally obtained as a residue of the electrolysis of water. Used as a source of deuterium, and in nuclear reactors for slowing down the neutrons which split the uranium atoms.\n\n(3) Other compounds made with deuterium, e.g., heavy acetylene, heavy methane, heavy acetic acids and heavy paraffin wax.\n\n(4) The isotopes of lithium known as lithium 6 or 7, and their compounds.\n\n(5) The isotope of carbon known as carbon 13, and its compounds.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n28.46 - 희토류(稀土류)금속ㆍ이트륨ㆍ스칸듐이나 이들 금속혼합물의 무기ㆍ유기 화합물\n\n2846.10 - 세륨화합물\n\n2846.90 - 기타\n\n이 호에는 이트륨ㆍ스칸듐이나 제2805호의 희토류(稀土類 : rare-earth) 금속[란타늄(lanthanum)ㆍ세륨(cerium)ㆍ프라세오디뮴(praseodymium)ㆍ니오디뮴(neodymium)ㆍ사마륨(samarium)ㆍ유로퓸(europium)ㆍ가돌리늄(gadolinium)ㆍ테르븀(terbium)ㆍ디스프로슘(dysprosium)ㆍ홀뮴(holmiu

"--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 28.47]\n\n28.47 Hydrogen peroxide, whether or not solidified with urea.\n\nHydrogen peroxide (H2O2) is obtained by electrolytic oxidation of sulphuric acid followed by distillation, or by treating barium or sodium peroxide or potassium persulphate with an acid. It is a colourless liquid with the appearance of ordinary water. It may have a syrupy consistency and corrodes the skin when concentrated. It is transported in carboys.\n\nHydrogen peroxide is very unstable in an alkaline medium, especially when exposed to heat or light. It nearly always contains small amounts of stabilisers (boric or citric acid, etc.) to prevent decomposition; such mixtures remain in this heading.\n\nThis heading also includes hydrogen peroxide, solidified with urea, whether or not stabilised.\n\nHydrogen peroxide is used for bleaching textiles, feathers, straw, sponges, ivory, hair, etc. It is also used for vat dyeing, for purifying water, for restoring old pictures, in photography and in medicine (as an antiseptic and haemostat).\n\nPresented as a medicament in measured doses or in forms or packings for retail sale, hydrogen peroxide falls in heading 30.04.\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n28.49 - 탄화물(화학적으로 단일한 것인지에 상관없다)\n\n2849.10 - 탄화칼슘\n\n2849.20 - 탄화규소\n\n2849.90 - 기타\n\n이 호에는 다음의 것을 분류한다.\n\n(A) 이성분탄화물(binary carbide) : 이것은 탄소보다 전기적 양성(electropositive)이 더 큰 다른 원소와 탄소의 화합물이다. 아세틸화물로 알려진 것도 이 호에 분류한다.\n\n잘 알려진 이성분탄화물은 다음과 같다.\n\n(1) 탄화칼슘(CaC2) : 순수 상태에서 투명하고 무색의 고체이며 불순물 상태에서는 유백색이나 회색이다. 물에 분해하여 아세틸렌을 생산하며 ; 아세틸렌(acetylene) 가스나 칼슘시안아미드 제조에 사용한다.\n\n(2) 탄화규소(SiC) :

"(d) 금속탄화물의 혼합물로 판(plate)ㆍ봉(stick)ㆍ팁(tip)이나 공구용과 유사한 것(제8209호)\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n[ENGLISH VERSION - 28.49]\n\n28.49 Carbides, whether or not chemically defined.\n\n2849.10 Of calcium\n\n2849.20 Of silicon\n\n2849.90 Other\n\nThis heading covers :\n\n(A) Binary carbides, which are compounds of carbon with another element more electropositive than carbon. Those known as acetylides are also classified in this heading.\n\nThe best known binary carbides are :\n\n(1) Calcium carbide (CaC2). A transparent, colourless solid in the pure state, opaque and grey when impure. Decomposed by water to produce acetylene; used for the production of the latter gas or of calcium cyanamide.\n\n(2) Silicon carbide (SiC) (carbon silicide). Obtained by treating carbon and silica in an electric furnace. Black crystals, lumps or shapeless masses, crushed or in grains. Fusible only with difficulty; resists chemical reagents; has a certain power of refraction, and is nearly as hard as diamond but rather brittle. Used extensively as an abrasive and as a refractory product; mixed with graphite it is used for lining electric furnaces or high temperature ovens. Also used for the manufacture of silicon. The heading excludes silicon carbide in the form of powder or grain on a backing of textile material, of paper, of paperboard or of other materials (heading 68.05), or in the form of grinding wheels, hand sharpening or polishing stones, etc. (heading 68.04).\n\n(3) Boron carbide (borocarbon). Obtained by treating graphite and boric acid in an electric furnace; hard brilliant blackish crystals. Used as abrasive, for boring rocks, in the manufacture of dies or electrodes.\n\n(4) Aluminium carbide (Al4C3). Obtained in an electric furnace by heating aluminium oxide with coke; transparent yellow crystals or flakes. Decomposed by water to produce methane.\n\n(5) Zirconium carbide (ZrC). "--------------------------------------------------\n\n28.52 - 무기나 유기의 수은화합물[화학적으로 단일한 것인지에 상관없으며 아말감(amalgam)은 제외한다]\n\n2852.10 - 화학적으로 단일한 것\n\n2852.90 - 기타\n\n이 호에는 아말감(amalgam)을 제외한 무기나 유기의 수은화합물(화학적으로 단일한 것인지에 상관없다)을 분류한다. 가장 일반적인 수은화합물은 아래와 같다.\n\n(1) 산화수은(mercury oxide) : 산화제이수은(HgO)은 수은의 가장 중요한 산화물이다. 밝은 적색 결정성 가루의 적색 산화물이나 짙은 오렌지-옐로우색 무정형 가루의 황색 산화물로 존재한다. 이러한 산화물은 독성이 있으며, 빛에 노출하면 흑색으로 변하고 이것은 해저 도료나 수은염의 조제에 사용하며 촉매로도 사용한다.\n\n(2) 염화수은(mercury chloride)\n\n(a) 염화제일수은(칼로멜)(Hg2Cl2) : 무정형의 덩어리ㆍ가루나 백색 결정으로 존재하며 ; 물에 녹지 않는다. 염화제일수은은 화공품(火工品, pyrotechnics)ㆍ자기공업 등에 사용한다.\n\n(b) 염화제이수은[승홍(昇汞, corrosive sublimate)](HgCl2) : 프리즘 모양인 결정체나 백색의 긴 바늘 모양의 결정이며, 물(특히 뜨거운 물)에 녹는다. ; 맹독성이다. 철 청동화제ㆍ목재방염 침투제ㆍ사진의 강화제ㆍ유기화학에서의 촉매로 사용하며 산화수은의 제조에도 사용한다.\n\n(3) 요드화수은(mercury iodide)\n\n(a) 요오드화제일수은(HgI이나 Hg2I2) : 가루로 보통 무정형이고 때로는 결정이며 ; 보통 황색, 때로는 녹색이나 적색이다. ; 물에 조금 녹으며 독성이 강하다. 유기 합성에 사용한다.\n\n(b) 요드화제이수은(HgI2) : 결정성 적색 가루로 거의 물에 녹지 않고 독성이 강하다. 사진(강화제)과 분석에 사용한다.\n\n(4) 황화수은(mercury sulphide) : 인조 황화수은(HgS)은 흑색이다. 승

Produced in a reverberatory furnace or in a crucible. Usually in yellowish-grey masses or in small, very brittle ingots of crystalline structure. The heading covers copper phosphide and master alloys of copper only if they contain more than 15 % by weight of phosphorus. Under this limit they fall generally in Chapter 74. Copper phosphide is a very good deoxidiser of copper, increasing the hardness of that metal; it improves the fluidity of molten metal, and is used in the manufacture of phosphor bronzes.\n\n(2) Calcium phosphide (Ca3P2). Small chestnut-coloured crystals or grey granular masses which, on contact with water, give off hydrogen phosphides which ignite spontaneously. Used with calcium carbide for naval signals(self-igniting flares for buoys).\n\n(3) Zinc phosphide (Zn3P2). Grey, poisonous powder with a vitreous fracture; gives off phosphine and deteriorates in moist air. Crystals; used as an antiseptic, as a local anaesthetic and also to relieve nasal congestion.\n\n(2) Cyclohexanol, methyl- and dimethylcyclohexanols are compounds with a characteristic odour like camphor. They are used as solvents for varnishes. Dimethylcyclohexanol is used in soap-making.\n\n(3) Sterols are alicyclic alcohols, saturated or unsaturated, the structure of which is derived from the hydrocarbon perhydro-1,2-cyclopentanophenanthrene, the hydroxyl group being linked to the 3-carbon, with a methyl group on the 10- and 13-carbons and a side chain of 8 to 10 carbon atoms linked to the 17-carbon. They exist abundantly both in the animal (zoosterols) and vegetable (phytosterols) kingdoms. The most important is cholesterol obtained mainly from the spinal cords of cattle and from wool grease; it is also obtained from bile, and as a by-product during the extraction of lecithin from egg-yolks. It is in the form of shiny, colourless tablets, insoluble in water.\n\nThis heading excludes ergosterol, found in fungi (mushrooms) and in spurred rye, a provitamin from which vitamin D2 is obt

Dimethylcyclohexanol is used in soap-making.\n\n(3) Sterols are alicyclic alcohols, saturated or unsaturated, the structure of which is derived from the hydrocarbon perhydro-1,2-cyclopentanophenanthrene, the hydroxyl group being linked to the 3-carbon, with a methyl group on the 10- and 13-carbons and a side chain of 8 to 10 carbon atoms linked to the 17-carbon. They exist abundantly both in the animal (zoosterols) and vegetable (phytosterols) kingdoms. The most important is cholesterol obtained mainly from the spinal cords of cattle and from wool grease; it is also obtained from bile, and as a by-product during the extraction of lecithin from egg-yolks. It is in the form of shiny, colourless tablets, insoluble in water.\n\nThis heading excludes ergosterol, found in fungi (mushrooms) and in spurred rye, a provitamin from which vitamin D2 is obtained by ultra-violet irradiation. Both ergosterol and vitamin D2 fall in heading 29.36
<truncated 16607 bytes>
"The most important is salicyl alcohol (saligenin) (HOC6H4CH2OH), white crystals, used in medicine as an analgesic and a febrifuge (antipyretic).\n\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n29.08 - 페놀이나 페놀알코올의 할로겐화유도체ㆍ술폰화유도체ㆍ니트로화유도체ㆍ니트로소화유도체\n\n- 할로겐화유도체와 이들의 염\n\n2908.11 - 펜타클로로페놀(ISO)\n\n2908.19 - 기타\n\n- 기타\n\n2908.91 - 디노셉(ISO)과 그 염\n\n2908.92 - 4,6-디니트로-오르토-크레졸[DNOC(ISO)]과 그 염\n\n2908.99 - 기타\n\n이들은 하나나 그 이상의 수소원자가 할로겐기(halgen group)․술폰기(sulpho group)(-SO3H), 니트로기(nitro group)(-NO2)나 니트로소기(nitroso group)(-NO)나 이들의 복합기와 치환하여 얻어지는 페놀(phenol)과 페놀알코올(phenol-alcohol)의 유도체이다.\n\n(A) 할로겐화(halogenated)유도체\n\n(1) 오르토클로로페놀(o-chlorophenol) : 강한 냄새를 지닌 액체\n\n(2) 메타클로로페놀(m-chlorophenol) : 무색결정체\n\n(3) 파라클로로페놀(p-chlorophenol) : 불쾌한 냄새를 지닌 괴상의 결정체이다.\n\n앞에서 설명한 세 가지의 물품은 유기 합성에 사용한다(예: 염료).\n\n(4) 파라-클로로-메타-크레졸(p-chloro-m-cresol)[4-클로로-3-메틸페놀(4-chloro-3-methylphenol)] : 무취의 소독제로서 물에 약간 녹으며 비누에 의해 용이하게 유화된다.\n\n(5) 클로로히드로퀴논(chlorohydroquinone)[클로로퀴놀(chloroquinol)]\n\n(B) 술폰화(sulphonated)유도체\n\n(1) 페놀술폰산(phenolsulphonic acid)(HOC6H4SO3H) : 페놀(phenol)을 술폰화(sulphonated)함으로써 얻어진다.\n\n(2) 나프톨술폰산(naphtholsuphonic acid) : 나프톨(naphthol)을 직접 술폰화(sulphonated)하거나 합성방법에 의하여 얻어진다. 이들은 염료의 제조에 사용하는 광범위한 화합물을 구성한다. 그 주요한 물품은 다음과 같다.\n\n(a) 1-나프톨-4-술폰산(1-naphthol-4-
